tbmm-2016-03-06sit02spe0126par0043	Mr. President, dear lawmakers, I salute you all again. I'd like to share with you the opinion and opinion of our party on the Finance Ministry budget. Dear friends, budgets are not just documents of numbers; on the contrary, they are very important, political, economic and administrative documents that reveal the government's policies. Budgets play an important role in maintaining balance among social classes and maintaining social peace. In addition, the most important indicators of political authorities' stances on labour, democracy, social rights and freedoms, human rights, different ethnic identities, belief groups and equal approaches towards different genders are budgets. When we consider it in this framework, we can say that the budget prepared by the Finance Ministry is not a peace budget and a budget based on social gender. Dear friends, this budget is an antidemocratic budget, regardless of the principle of participation. This budget would not have been shaped as a war budget if society's proposals had already been taken in the process of drafting a budget. It is possible to say that the 2016 budget is a sanctioned document of the swamp that the Kurds were stuck in to destroy and suppress their struggle to achieve a status in both our country and especially Syria. Dear friends, especially after the June 7th elections, the budget was a war budget, with the increase in war-returning goods and service purchases. In the 2015 budget, the purchases of goods and services were estimated to be $44.4.4 billion, as of the end of the year. In this case, it would not be wrong to say that nine liras of each $100 of the budget is spent on purchases of goods and services. If you examine the contents of these services, it can be seen that the amount of the pen under the title of defence and security is about one third of these purchases. The total share of these expenditures is 2.6, which is 12.7 billion, within the central management budget. Again, a significant percentage of defence and security and service purchases -- this 73 per cent -- belongs to the Ministry of National Defence, while in the 2nd place, the General Staff of the Gendarme will be seen as 15 per cent and 12 per cent of the Security Directorate. Especially after the June 7th elections, there's a war that's been started to take power on its own, and it's a conflicting environment. Yes, that's why the deficit in the budget has been cleared by allowing the Finance Minister to transfer between funds. In fact, this also makes no sense to budget talks because it reveals that the Parliament's spending authority has been spent on more than its spending power, which is contrary to Article 163 of the Constitution. As of September 2015, the presidency, the Prime Minister's office -- they have covered funds, known as -- the budgets of the National Defence, Internal Affairs and Transport Ministry have significantly exceeded initial budgets. The reason for the overpayment is because the government's return to the concept of war is very closely reflected in the budgets. For example, the budget for the Ministry of National Education, unfortunately, has been transferred by the Ministry of Finance to other ministries, not spent training by the Ministry of National Education, where they are educated in single-class schools. Another remarkable issue is that under the name of the special account, a covert grant-like application was implemented by this power. The temporary budget for 2016 included private accounts for Foreign Affairs, Development, Family and Social Policy, National Education, Ministry of the European Union, and the budgets for the Ministry of Disaster and Emergency Management, which ministers could use without subjecting the current public spending law, accounting and control system. Thus, it is possible that spending with these private accounts will be kept out of Parliamentary control. Again, when we look at the distribution of the revenues of the 2016 budget, 84.8 per cent of the revenues are from taxes, and the rest are from higher increases in prices of tuition, electricity, gas, fines, and other state revenues. The burden of taxes that make up the majority is unfortunately above the working classes. This shows to all of us that the financing of the state-owned neoliberal marketing transformation, rising militantism, authoritarianisation and the monotheistic understanding of power is essentially being carried out by taxes collected from the public. The fact that taxes are on the backs of workers is also understood by the way taxes are taken. Because the income tax rate of institutions and direct taxes on wealth is up to 30 per cent, while a major 70 per cent is provided from indirect taxes such as VAT and EVTV. As inflation increases, these taxes also increase, and in times of inflation, the burden on the public is increasing. Again, there are distortions in the implementation of these taxes. For example, the VAT rate for public goods services such as meat, milk, health, education is 8 per cent, while those who bring precious, semi-valued raw stones to Turkey via Istanbul do not pay VAT. Also, as the public will recall, the recently released bag of legislation has been removed from importing uncut gems of valuable, semi-valued stones. Dear friends, Turkey's main income is taxes. Only 10 per cent of these taxes are being used by local governments, and major cities such as Ankara, Istanbul are taking a share, which brings in inequality. The AKP says the Metropolitan Law strengthens municipalities and local governments, increasing the authorities and responsibilities of the municipalities. It is clear, however, that it is not enough to strengthen one with the use of 10 percent of resources. Dear lawmakers, democracy means that the masses have words and decisions, not to go to the polls every four or five years and vote. The definition of democracies with pure polls, as in AKP's rule, is an effort to prevent the rise of public will and demands. Democracy, in the local community, is the speech of society about its present and future life, its participation in decision-making processes, its assessment of its consequences. Democracys where people participate in the process through neighborhoods, districts, districts, youth, women's parliaments, civil society organisations are real democracies. This model, described as strengthening local democracy or ▪ self-government, will contribute to the resolution of faith, women, youth, other problems, especially the Kurdish problem, and to the democratisation of Turkey. In this model, a more equitable distribution of revenues can be carried out between the regions of the region, based on the needs and properties of the districts -- adding up the area under 25-26 -- with a geographical, cultural and economic features, according to the properties of the districts -- from Van (Izmir) to Samsun (Izanne) to Adana, from Diyarbakir to İstanbul. In this administrative arrangement, constitutional amendments must be made to remove the reservations in the conditions for autonomy in Europe's Local Governments and to determine what the powers and responsibilities of these regions will be. In such a model, the public participates in the budget production process, how to distribute, by revealing what their needs are, what their priorities should be. As part of recent improvements, the Finance Ministry has moved from 2004 to 2004, under central administration administration administrations to analytic budget classification in line with international standards. The new budget rating allows for analysis of the impact of state activities within the economy and comparisons with international data. The new budget rating and the relevant accounting system began to be implemented in a way that included local administrations as of 2006. With Law number 5217 dated July 14, 2004, special income and special funding practices have been changed in order to strengthen budget discipline and budget rights. The Public Financial Management and Control Act 5018, which structured our public financial management system in accordance with international standards, has been in effect since 2006. In an effort to establish revenue policies and implement the established policies, the Head of the Finance Administration and the General Directorate of Finance Policy were established, and their structures were largely completed. Mr. President, the valued lawmakers are the income budget, the second leg of the budget, and Article 2 of the law. It determines the estimated amount of budget revenues. Whether the estimated sums are reached or not is directly related to the economic and political conditions of that year. Budget work is based on first-referencing costs to meet mandatory needs. Once the costs are identified, they try to provide suitable income. The difference between income and travel creates a budget deficit. In order to minimize budget deficits, it is clear that we are obliged to maintain a firm stance in our finance policy and to improve our policies to increase revenues. Again, the establishment of justice and efficiency in taxation, the fight against the informal economy, the continuation of fiscal discipline, the incentive of employment and investments, the development of competition, the reduction of regional and social development differences are the main objectives of our income policies. Mr. President, valued lawmakers are the revenues of central management revenues, general budget revenues, private budgetary administrations, regulatory and auditory institutions. These debts are often discussed for what purposes, for what resources they are provided, for what burdens they pose to the state and citizens, for how they are used and managed, and for the impact on the country's economy. In that sense, I'd like to share some numbers with you: According to data previously released by the Treasury Undersecretary, Turkey's debt supply data is as follows at the end of the third quarter of 2015: Turkey's gross foreign debt stock is around 406 billion dollars at the end of the third quarter. Turkey's net foreign debt stock is 240.3 billion dollars at the end of the third quarter. Turkey's public net debt stock is 143.4 billion euros at the end of the third quarter. Turkey's EU-defined stockpile of general management debt is 654.8 billion TL as of the end of the third quarter. The gross domestic debt was 155.2 billion dollars in 2002 and was $412.4 billion at the end of May 2014. As of the end of 2002, Turkey's gross foreign debt was $129.6 billion, and the gross foreign debt was $386.8 billion at the end of May 2014. The highest increase in external debt was in the private sector. In 2002, private sector foreign debt, $43 billion, rose to $265 billion at the end of May 2014. According to this, the total domestic and external debt rose from 222 billion dollars in 2002 to $581 billion in 2014. The budget deficit is growing, unfortunately. Despite total taxes and privatisation revenues during the AKP period, his debts both increased and in 12 years -- as of 2015 -- gave more than 273 billion euro deficits.
